# SOURCE_FILE: CHEY-2023-05-02_TotalExtraction_62759738.pdf
PIPELINE EASEMENT 
 
BE IT KNOWN BE ALL PERSONS PRESENT that City of Cheyenne, Wyoming, a 
Municipal Corporation, whose address is 2101 O’Neil Avenue Room 101, Cheyenne, WY 82001, 
for itself and its heirs, administrators, successors, and assigns (collectively “Grantor”), for and in 
consideration of the sum of One Hundred and no/100ths Dollars ($100.00) and other good and 
valuable consideration, the receipt and sufficiency of which is hereby acknowledged and 
confessed, has granted, conveyed, sold, and warranted, and does hereby grant, convey, sell, and 
warrant unto Samson Exploration, LLC, a Texas limited liability company whose address is 110 
West 7th Street, Suite 2000, Tulsa, Oklahoma 74119 and its affiliates, successors, and assigns 
(collectively “Grantee”) an easement, servitude, license, and right to use (the “Easement”) in, 
upon, across, through, over, and under the lands described on the attached Exhibit “A” located in 
Laramie County, Wyoming (the “Lands”), as said Easement is more fully described as follows: 
 
 
Pipeline — The right to use a thirty feet (30’) wide strip across the Lands to survey, lay, 
construct, install, operate, inspect, protect, alter, maintain, improve, repair, change the size of, 
relocate, add, replace, remove, and/or abandon in place one or more pipelines and all valves, 
fittings, devices for controlling electrolysis and/or cleaning pipeline interiors, and/or other 
necessary appurtenances above and below ground, including suitable markers to mark the location 
of the pipeline(s), for the purposes of transportation of oil, gas, petroleum products, water and/or 
any other liquids, gases, or substances which can be transported through pipelines.  This right shall 
be expanded from time to time during any period(s) of construction, maintenance, or repair to a 
width of sixty feet (60’) for so long as such use is reasonably necessary for the operations being 
conducted, and shall revert to the permanent width set forth above upon completion 
thereof.  Grantee shall maintain the uppermost part of the underground portions of said pipelines 
at least thirty-six inches (36”) below the surface of the ground, but this limitation shall not apply 
to any portion of the pipelines or other equipment installed above the surface. 
 
The approximate location(s) of the Easement associated with the pipeline are set out on the 
plat attached hereto and marked as Exhibit “B”.  Grantee may, in its sole judgment, replace the 
preliminary plat attached hereto as Exhibit  “B” with a final or as-built surveyed plat depicting the 
actual boundaries or centerlines of said pipeline by recording a designation of easement location 
(“Designation”) in the county in which the applicable Lands are situated, and any such Designation 
and any exhibits thereto shall supersede and/or replace that portion of the Exhibit “B” attached 
hereto which has been re-surveyed and re-platted as provided herein.  Upon request, Grantee shall 
forward a copy of such Designation to Grantor. 
 
The rights granted herein include the right of ingress and egress over and across any 
existing or future trails, roads, or canals on the Lands which were not constructed by Grantee to 
carry out and fulfill all purposes described herein.  Grantee acknowledges that Grantor intends to 
install a gravel trail on the Lands, and that said trail may at some time become a paved trail.  
Grantee shall repair any damages to such trails, roads, or canals caused by Grantee’s use to as close 
to their condition prior to Grantee’s use as is practicable. 
 
9:29 am, Apr 19 2023

 
The rights granted herein include the right to construct, install, operate, maintain, repair, 
replace, add to, and remove electric, communication, and/or control lines either above or below 
the Lands as may be beneficial to Grantee’s operations.  
 
Without prior, written consent of the Grantee, Grantor shall not construct or permit 
construction within the boundaries of the Easement reflected on Exhibit “B” or a Designation, and 
Grantee shall have the right to prevent the construction therein and the right to remove therefrom, 
any and all types and sizes of houses, barns, buildings, structures, permanent impoundments of 
water, and natural or man-made obstructions, including but not limited to trees, brush, roots, and 
other growth. Grantor shall not, nor permit third parties to, change the grade of the land or remove 
the cover over the Pipeline or excavate on or near such portions of the Easement without prior 
written consent of the Grantee, which consent shall not be unreasonably withheld provided same 
does not present an operational or safety issue for Grantee. 
 
Except as otherwise set forth herein, the consideration paid hereunder includes payment 
for all damages to the Lands, and Grantor hereby acknowledges receipt and sufficiency of said 
payment as full and complete settlement for and as a release of all claims for loss, damage, or 
injury to the Lands arising out of the operations contemplated hereunder.  Grantee shall have the 
right from time to time to cut or clear trees, brush, and other obstructions on the Lands that might 
interfere with the operation, access to, or maintenance of the easements granted herein or any 
facilities or equipment thereon relating to the rights granted herein.  This Easement shall in no way 
diminish, lessen, or remove any rights now or hereafter held by Grantee, its successors or assigns, 
by and through any and all oil, gas, and mineral lease(s) or any other agreements that cover all or 
any portion of the Lands subject to this Easement. 
 
Grantor represents and warrants title to the herein granted Easement unto Grantee, its 
successors and assigns, and does hereby agree to forever defend all and singular such interest unto 
Grantee, its successors and assigns, against any person whomsoever claiming or to claim the 
same.  This Easement and all of the terms, provisions and obligations hereof shall be covenants 
running with the Lands and shall inure to the benefit of and be binding upon Grantor and Grantee 
and their respective successors, heirs, and assigns. 
 
Upon the termination of this Easement, Grantee shall perform all work necessary to purge 
or remove all flammable or explosive substances contained within and to cap or seal any pipelines 
buried pursuant to this Easement in accordance with all applicable laws, regulations, and sound 
engineering practices.  All pipe left in place shall then become and remain the sole property of 
Grantor to the exclusion of any claim by Grantee or any party claiming by, through, or under 
Grantee. At such time Grantor shall accept, and shall be deemed to have accepted, all such 
pipelines “AS-IS” and “WHERE-IS”, with all faults and defects, and without warranty of any kind 
whatsoever, and assumes and agrees to bear all risks and responsibilities related thereto. 
 
GRANTEE SHALL INDEMNIFY GRANTOR AGAINST ANY CLAIMS, DAMAGES, 
DEMANDS, LIABILITIES, AND COSTS TO THE EXTENT ARISING FROM OR RELATED 
TO THE NEGLIGENCE OR MISCONDUCT OF GRANTEE OR ITS EMPLOYEES, AGENTS, 
CONTRACTORS, OR INVITEES IN THE COURSE OF THEIR EXERCISE OF RIGHTS 

 
GRANTED BY THIS EASEMENT, BUT NOT TO THE EXTENT CAUSED BY GRANTOR, 
OR ITS EMPLOYEES, AGENTS, CONTRACTORS, OR INVITEES. 
 
Grantor hereby enters into this Agreement and receives full consideration on behalf of 
Grantor and any surface tenants, surface lessees, permitees, and licensees on the Lands, and their 
respective successors, heirs, and assigns. Grantor is responsible for sharing such consideration 
with any such individual(s) or entity(ies), to the extent they are entitled to compensation related to 
Grantee’s activities. 
 
The rights granted herein are non-exclusive, and Grantor reserves and retains the right to 
convey similar rights to such other persons as Grantor may deem proper; provided, however, that 
all such conveyances shall be subject to Grantee’s rights, and Grantee shall not be unreasonably 
disturbed in the use and enjoyment of the rights granted herein. 
 
In the event Grantee shall be in default or breach of any of the terms of this Easement, 
Grantor shall give written notice to Grantee of such default or breach.  Grantee shall then have 
ninety (90) days within which to commence to remedy any alleged default.   
 
Any notice provided or permitted to be given in this instrument must be in writing and may 
be given by depositing the notice in the United States mail, postage prepaid, certified with return 
receipt requested, and addressed to the party to be notified.  Notice deposited in the mail in the

MEMORANDUM OF EASEMENT 
 
State: 
Wyoming

Laramie 
 
Grantor: 
City of Cheyenne, Wyoming, a Municipal Corporation

Effective Date:

Notice is hereby given that the Grantor(s) named above have executed and delivered to the 
Grantee named above an instrument (the “Easement”) in which Grantor conveyed unto Grantee 
and its affiliates, successors, and assigns an easement, servitude, license, and right to use in, upon, 
across, through, over, and under the following lands (the “Lands”) located in the county and state 
named above: 
 
Township 13 North, Range 66 West, 6th P.M. 
Section 1: Part NE/4 & Part S/2 
Laramie County, Wyoming 
 
The Easement consists of the following components: 
 
A non-exclusive term easement for pipelines across a 30’ wide strip of land (or wider 
during construction or repair) with a centerline depicted on the plat attached hereto as Exhibit 
“B”. 
Grantee has the right to unilaterally file a notice replacing the preliminary plat attached 
hereto as Exhibit “B” with a final or as-built surveyed plat depicting the actual boundaries and/or 
centerlines of the easements granted by the Easement, and any such notice and revised plat shall 
supersede and replace the Exhibit “B” attached hereto.   
 
The term easements are each effective for five (5) years following initial construction and 
for so long thereafter as some or all of the same shall be used for the purposes which are granted 
in the Easement with no cessation of all such use for more than five (5) consecutive years. 
 
This Memorandum of Easement is executed by Grantor and Grantee and placed of record 
for the purpose of placing all persons on notice of the existence of the Easement, which is not 
being recorded.  Executed copies of the Easement are maintained by Grantor and Grantee, and the 
full terms of the Easement shall be binding on the parties and are incorporated herein despite not 
being included in this Memorandum. This Memorandum may be executed as one document or in 
several partially executed counterparts, and the original and counterpart shall be construed together 
and shall constitute one instrument.  
 
This Memorandum is signed by Grantor and Grantee as of the date of the acknowledgment 
of their signatures on the following page, but is effective for all purposes as of the Effective Date 
stated above.

City Contract No. ____________ 
 
PROFESSIONAL SERVICES AGREEMENT 
for 
Landfill Leachate System Design, Modification and 
Maintenance Contract 
 
This Professional Services Agreement (“Agreement”) is made and entered into by and 
between the City of Cheyenne, located at 2101 O’Neil Avenue, Cheyenne, Wyoming 82001 
(“City”), and Terracon Consultants, Inc., located at 1901 Sharp Point Drive, Suite C, Fort 
Collins. Colorado (“Consultant”).  This Agreement shall become effective upon the date of the 
last signature affixed hereto. 
 
 
RECITALS 
 
In consideration of the mutual covenants and obligations herein expressed, it is agreed by 
and between the parties as follows: 
 
1. 
SCOPE OF WORK 
The Consultant agrees to provide services as described for the above-mentioned project, 
in accordance with the Scope of Work attached hereto as Exhibit A, incorporated by 
reference and expressly made a part hereof. 
 
2. 
TIME OF PERFORMANCE 
The work included in this Scope of Work will be completed by October 31, 2024, as 
stated in Exhibit A. 
 
3. 
COMPENSATION 
In consideration of the services to be performed pursuant to this Agreement, the 
Consultant will bill the City and the City agrees to pay Consultant a not to exceed amount 
of Sixty One Thousand, Four Hundred Dollars ($61,400.00) as stated in Exhibit A.  
 
4. 
CONSULTANT RESPONSIBILITY 
In providing services under this Agreement, the Consultant shall perform in a manner 
consistent with that degree of care and skill ordinarily exercised by members of the same 
profession currently practicing under similar circumstances. Upon notice to the 
Consultant, the Consultant will, without additional compensation, correct those services 
not meeting such a standard. 
 
5. 
COMPLIANCE WITH LAWS 
The Parties agree that they will perform their obligations as provided in this Agreement 
in accordance with all applicable laws and ordinances. 
 
 
10:29 am, May 15 2023

 
2
6. 
DRUG-FREE WORKPLACE 
In compliance with the Drug-Free Workplace Act of November 1988, the City of 
Cheyenne has established an Alcohol and Controlled Substance Policy that pertains to 
alcohol and drug usage by City Employees. All architects, engineers, and other 
consultants under contract with City of Cheyenne, and their employees and 
sub-consultants, are required to comply with the provisions of the City’s Alcohol and 
Controlled Substance Policy for drug and/or alcohol usage on City property or other sites 
occupied by the Consultant while performing the duties and responsibilities of the 
Agreement. It is the responsibility of the Consultant to familiarize themselves with the 
requirements of this policy and to inform all their employees and sub-consultants of the 
requirements and ensure their compliance. If the Consultant, their employees or 
sub-consultants are found in violation of this policy, the Agreement may be terminated. 
 
7. 
INDEMNIFICATION/HOLD HARMLESS 
The Consultant agrees to indemnify, hold harmless, and defend the City from and against 
any and all liabilities, claims, penalties, forfeitures, and suits, and the cost and expenses 
incident thereto, including reasonable attorney’s fees, which may hereafter arise as a 
result the performance of the Consultant’s duties, including death or bodily injury to any 
person, destruction or damage to any property, contamination of or adverse effects on the 
environment, or any violation of governmental laws, regulations, or orders to the extent 
caused by (1) the Consultant’s breach of any term or provision of this Agreement; or (2) 
any negligent or wrongful act, error, or omission by the Consultant, or its employees or 
sub-consultants in the performance of this Agreement.  The Consultant acknowledges 
that it may incur a financial obligation to the City pursuant to the terms of this paragraph. 
 
8. 
INSURANCE REQUIREMENTS 
The Consultant must provide proof of the following insurance coverages: 
 
 
Commercial General Liability Insurance 
For claims arising out of bodily injury, illness, or death, or from damage to or destruction 
of property of others, including loss or use thereof, with minimum limits of $1,000,000 
per occurrence, $2,000,000 aggregate for the entire term of the Agreement. 
 
 
Workers’ Compensation 
Workers’ compensation coverage shall be in effect for the entire term of the Agreement, 
as required by Wyoming law, for all employees or agents providing services under this 
Agreement.  Consultant shall provide the City with proof of workers’ compensation or 
employer’s liability insurance coverage. 
 
 
Professional Liability Insurance 
The Consultant shall provide proof of professional liability insurance or errors and 
omissions liability insurance in an amount not less than $500,000 to protect the City from 
any and all claims arising from the Consultant’s negligence in the performance of duties 
under this Agreement.  The City prefers that this liability insurance coverage be 
provided pursuant to an “occurrence” policy.  

 
3
 
If this coverage is provided pursuant to a “claims made” policy: 
 
 
a. 
Consultant shall, concurrently with the execution of this Agreement, 
provide the City with a certificate of insurance demonstrating that such 
coverage is or shall be in effect at the time the Consultant begins the 
provision of services under this Agreement; and 
 
 
b. 
In the event the Consultant’s services extend into a future policy period, 
the Consultant shall, prior to the policy expiration date, provide the City 
with a new certificate of insurance demonstrating that such coverage is or 
shall be in effect during all periods of time that Consultant will provide 
services under this Agreement; and 
 
 
c. 
Consultant shall maintain said “claims made” coverage for a period of five 
(5) years following the last date that Consultant has provided services 
under this Agreement; and 
 
 
d. 
In the event the Consultant or the insurer terminates “claims made” 
coverage prior to the expiration of the periods provided in subparagraphs 
a., b., or c. of this paragraph, the Consultant shall provide to the City 
advance written notification of the termination of said coverage and shall 
provide the City with an endorsement for an extended reporting period 
(“tail coverage”) which shall be in effect for a period of time not less than 
five (5) years following the last date that Consultant has provided services 
under this Agreement.  
 
 
Additional Insurance Information 
 
The Consultant shall name the City of Cheyenne as an Additional Insured by 
endorsement on its insurance policies and shall provide the City with a copy of the 
endorsements.  This requirement does not apply to workers’ compensation and 
professional liability insurance policies. 
 
Consultant shall provide the City with certificates of insurance acknowledging the 
above-stated coverages prior to beginning any work under this Agreement. 
 
It is understood and agreed that these policies are primary and not contributory. All 
policies required under this Agreement shall be in effect for the duration of the 
Agreement.  It shall be an affirmative obligation upon Consultant to immediately notify 
in writing the city risk manager, city clerk, and city attorney of any fact, circumstance, or 
occurrence that has resulted in or may result in the cancellation of, or substantive change 
to any insurance coverage required by this Agreement, and failure to do so shall be 
construed to be a breach of this Agreement. 
 
If requested by the City, the Consultant shall provide the City with copies of insurance 
policies and/or policy endorsements listing the City of Cheyenne as an additional insured.  
The City’s failure to request or review such policies, endorsements, or certificates shall 
not affect the City’s rights or Consultant’s obligation hereunder. 
 

 
4
Any insurance company providing coverage under this Agreement shall have a minimum 
A. M. Best rating of A- (excellent). 
 
9. 
MINORITY AND DISADVANTAGED BUSINESS ENTERPRISES 49 C.F.R. Part 26 
All parties to this Agreement assure that no person will be excluded from participation in, 
denied the benefits of, or otherwise discriminated against, in connection with the award 
and performance of this Agreement on the grounds of age, race, color, disability, national 
origin, or sex. 
 
10. 
MONTHLY REPORT 
With every monthly billing, Consultant shall provide the City Representative with a 
written statement of the status of the work with respect to the Scope of Work, time sheets, 
and work schedule. Failure to provide the required monthly report will delay processing 
of any payment request until the report is submitted. 
 
11. 
INDEPENDENT CONSULTANT 
The Consultant shall function as an independent consultant for the purposes of this 
Agreement. The Consultant shall assume sole responsibility for any debts or liabilities 
that may be incurred by the Consultant in fulfilling the terms of this Agreement. Nothing 
in this Agreement shall be interpreted as authorizing the Consultant or its agents or 
employees to act as an agent or representative of or on behalf of the City or to incur any 
obligation of any kind on the behalf of the City. 
 
12. 
TAXES 
The Consultant agrees to pay all valid taxes, excises, license fees, permit fees, bills, 
debts, and obligations incurred by and in connection with its operations under this 
Agreement. 
 
13. 
DEFAULT 
Each and every term and condition in this Agreement shall be deemed to be a material 
element of the Agreement. In the event either party should fail or refuse to perform 
according to the terms of this Agreement, such party may be declared in default. 
 
14. 
REMEDIES 
In the event a party has been declared in default, such defaulting party shall be allowed a 
period of fifteen (15) days within which to cure the default.  In the event the default 
remains uncorrected, the non-defaulting party declaring default may elect to: 
 
 
a. 
Terminate the Agreement and seek damages, which damages shall not 
exceed the contract amount; or 
 
 
b. 
Treat the Agreement as continuing and require specific performance. 
 
15. 
TERM AND TERMINATION 
The term of this Agreement shall be from date of execution to May 31, 2024.  The 
parties may by mutual written agreement renew or extend this Agreement. 

 
5
The City may, without cause, and upon thirty days’ written notice to the Consultant, 
terminate this Agreement in whole or in part at any time, for the City’s convenience. 
Upon receipt of such notice, the Consultant shall: 
 
 
a. 
Discontinue all services affected; and 
 
 
b. 
Deliver to the City within five (5) days all data, drawings, specifications, 
reports, estimates, summaries, and such other information and materials as 
may have been accumulated by the Consultant in performing this 
Agreement, whether completed or in process. 
 
 
c. 
In the event of termination for convenience, the City will pay the 
Consultant for accepted work done and expenses incurred to the date of 
termination. Such acceptance shall not be unreasonably withheld. 
 
16. 
ADDITIONAL REMEDIES 
In the event the Consultant fails to strictly perform in accordance with this Agreement, 
the City may elect to correct the deficiencies and charge the Consultant. In the event of 
default of any of the conditions by either party which shall require the party not in default 
to commence legal or equitable action against the defaulting party each party shall bear 
its own costs and expenses, including without limitation, attorney’s fees.  
 
17. 
GOVERNING LAW, JURISDICTION AND VENUE 
The construction, interpretation, and enforcement of this Agreement shall be governed by 
the laws of the State of Wyoming.  The courts of the State of Wyoming shall have 
jurisdiction over any action arising out of this Agreement and over the parties, and the 
venue shall be the First Judicial District, Laramie County, Wyoming. 
 
18. 
GOVERNMENTAL IMMUNITY 
The City of Cheyenne, along with its officials and employees, does not waive its

available to it as a sovereign or governmental entity pursuant to Wyo. Stat. § 1-39-101, et 
seq., and all other relevant state and federal law. Designations of venue, choice of law, 
enforcement actions, and similar provisions should not be constructed as a waiver of 
governmental immunity. The parties agree that any ambiguity in this Agreement shall not 
be strictly construed, either against or for either party, except that any ambiguity as to 
governmental or sovereign immunity shall be construed in favor of governmental 
immunity. 
 
19. 
MONITORING ACTIVITIES 
The City shall have the right to monitor all activities related to this Agreement that are

OWNERSHIP OF DOCUMENTS AND WORK PRODUCT

Dear Mr. Quattrini:
Terracon Consultants, Inc. (Terracon) appreciates the opportunity to submit this proposal to the City
of Cheyenne (client) to perform system design and maintenance associated with the leachate
discharge systems at the above-referenced site. The following sections provide an outline of the
project, Terracon’s scope of services, including schedule, and compensation.
Scope of Services
(see Section 2 below)
Task 1 – Quarterly and semi-annual O&M of NT-3, NT-4, NT-
5, Cell 1 and Cell 2 with corresponding reporting
Task 2 - Leachate Recovery Pump Installation (NT-4 and Cell
2 )
Task 3 - Out of Scope services
Task 4 - Shelter Design for Cell 1 and Cell 2 Pads
Task 5 - Concrete Vault Raise Design
Compensation
Task 1a - $6,400 per event
Task 1b - $3,900 per event
Task 2 - $4,500 per event
Task 3 - $15,000 (time and materials)
Task 4 - $5,250 (time and materials)
Task 5 - $3,250 (time and materials)
Estimated Total – $61,400
1.0 PROJECT INFORMATION
The Happy Jack Landfill site includes leachate collection pumping systems that includes a total of
seven wells, NT-1 through NT-5, Cell 1 and Cell 2, with varying well casing diameters and depths.
Leachate collection wells NT-3 through NT-5, Cell 1 and Cell 2 are each equipped with a submersible
leachate recovery pump, digital flow meter, pressure transducer and pump controller. The pump
controllers use fluid level data from pressure transducers to activate the recovery pump when the

Proposal for Leachate System Design, Modification and Maintenance
City of Cheyenne Happy Jack Landfill I Cheyenne, Wyoming
Terracon Proposal No. P20227120 I April 28, 2023
2
leachate fluid level reaches approximately one foot above the landfill liner depth. Upon pump
activation, NT-3 and NT-4 are pumped to a manifold that is then discharged to the evaporation pond.
Upon pump activation at NT-5, leachate is pumped to an on-site evaporation pond. Upon pump
activation, liquid in Cell 1 and Cell 2 is pumped to a manifold that is then discharged to the
evaporation pond.
As of December 2022, Terracon field personnel verified that well NT-1 and NT-2 were intact but did
not contain any pump, effluent piping, or pump controller. Additionally, confined space entry is
required for inspection and cleaning of the pump associated with NT-5, which is reflected in the scope
of services, schedule, and compensation.
2.0 SCOPE OF SERVICES
2.1
Quarterly and Semi-Annual Scheduled Routine Operation and
Maintenance
At your request, Terracon will conduct quarterly and semi-annual maintenance and reporting, or as
requested by the City of Cheyenne. At this time, maintenance will include:
Quarterly Maintenance at Wells NT-1 and NT-4
Manually gauge the fluid level in wells NT-1 through NT-4
Gauge the total depth of each well and calculate the standing fluid column in NT-1 through NT-4
and evaluate the presence of sediments
Record the fluid level reading on the pump controller and review the controller settings (NT-3 and
NT-4)
Record pump run time (NT-3 and NT-4)
Record flow meter reading (NT-3 and NT-4)
Inspect effluent hoses and clear of any debris or ice (NT-3 and NT-4)
Record the pump setting depth and remove the pumps in each well (NT-3 and NT-4)
Visually inspect each pump, pressure transducer and the wiring connections (NT-3 and NT-4)
Test leachate pumps (NT-3 and NT-4). If a test indicates that a pump has failed or cannot be
repaired in the field, Terracon will contact client regarding parts, cost, and schedule
Quarterly Maintenance at Well NT-5
Record pump run time
Record flow meter reading
Inspect effluent hoses located above-grade and clear of any debris or ice
Test leachate pump. If a test indicates that a pump has failed or cannot be repaired in the field,
Terracon will contact client regarding parts, cost, and schedule
Semi-annual maintenance at NT-5 to include the following:
Confined space entry and atmospheric monitoring using a 4-gas meter
Record the pump setting depth and remove the pump
Remove and visually inspect the pump, pressure transducer and the wiring connections
Clean and test the pump. If a test indicates that a pump has failed or cannot be repaired in the
field, Terracon will contact the client

Proposal for Leachate System Design, Modification and Maintenance
City of Cheyenne Happy Jack Landfill I Cheyenne, Wyoming
Terracon Proposal No. P20227120 I April 28, 2023
3
Quarterly Maintenance at Cell 1 and Cell 2
Record the pump run time and the flow meter reading for
Record the fluid level reading on the pump controller and review the controller settings
Inspect effluent hoses and clear of any debris or ice, replace if damaged
Record the pump setting depth and remove the pump
Visually inspect pump, pressure transducer and the wiring connections
Clean the surface of the pumps, pump intakes and pressure transducers,
Test the leachate pumps. If a test indicates that a pump has failed or cannot be repaired in the
field, Terracon will contact client
Following each site visit, Terracon will submit a letter report summarizing the field activities including:
Maintenance, inspections, and cleaning conducted
Depth readings collected
Testing conducted
Repairs, cleaning, or additional items conducted
Recommendations for additional maintenance, as needed.
2.2
Pump Install and Troubleshooting
Terracon will mobilize to the site to install the submersible leachate recovery pumps from NT-4 and
Cell 2, and includes the following:
Mobilization to the site.
Installation of the of the leachate recovery pumps at leachate collection wells NT-4 and Cell 2
upon receipt of the pumps and wiring from EPG. These materials will be purchased by the client.
Following the installation of the pumps, the electrical wiring will be reconnected, and the pump will
be tested.
Terracon assumes the two leachate recovery pumps (NT-4 and Cell 2) will be installed in one
mobilization to the site. Installation and testing will not require more than one day on-site.
2.3
Out of Scope Services (Annual Contract)
In addition to the above referenced scheduled routine maintenance: additional maintenance, repairs
and/or equipment may be recommended or required for system operation during the annual contract
period, including non-routine site visits and emergency site visits. The additional out of scope services
may include, but is not limited to, the following:
Pump replacement including testing and reprogramming to verify proper working condition (City of
Cheyenne to provide replacement pumps, Terracon can provide cost estimates for pumps and
equipment as requested)
Pressure transducer replacement including calibration and reprogramming (City of Cheyenne to
provide replacement parts, Terracon can provide cost estimates for pressure transducers and
equipment as requested)
Effluent hose replacement
Additional cleanout or repairs of piping and equipment
Provide 72-hour on-call emergency troubleshooting

Proposal for Leachate System Design, Modification and Maintenance
City of Cheyenne Happy Jack Landfill I Cheyenne, Wyoming
Terracon Proposal No. P20227120 I April 28, 2023
4
2.4
Shelter Design for Cell 1 and Cell 2
The leachate collection piping for the new landfill cells is above ground. The facility would like to have
a building constructed that encompasses leachate collection piping that is located above ground. They
would like Terracon to design a building/shed around the piping that can be heated to keep the piping
from freezing, while still allowing access to the well cleanout, piping, and sample ports. The design
could also be used on future cells at the site. Terracon will need the facilities existing permit and as-
built information for the new cells. A site visit will be required to acquire the necessary information for
the base dimensions, piping locations, and control panel location. There will be an initial draft layout to
be submitted for review and recommendation by site personnel to tailor the structure to their specific
needs.
2.5
Concrete Vault Raise Design
The older landfill cells use concrete vaults. As the current capacity of these previously constructed
landfill cells is being utilized, these concrete vaults (NT-3 and NT-4) will need to be raised. The facility
would like Terracon to design the requirements to raise these concrete vaults. Terracon will perform a
site visit to acquire any information for these concrete vaults. Terracon will need to review the permit
and as-built construction information that pertains to these concrete vaults.
2.6
Health and Safety
Terracon has a 100% commitment to the safety of all its employees. As such, and in accordance with
our Incident and Injury Free® safety culture, Terracon will update the existing safety plan to be used
by our personnel during field services. Prior to commencement of on-site activities, Terracon will hold
a meeting to review health and safety needs for this specific project, including confined space entry
protocol. At this time, we anticipate performing fieldwork in a USEPA Level D work uniform consisting
of hard hats, safety glasses, protective gloves, and steel-toed boots. Additional safety procedures will
be utilized for confined space entry. It may become necessary to upgrade this level of protection, at
additional cost, while maintenance activities are being conducted, if chemical constituents are
encountered in vapors, soils, leachate, or groundwater that may pose an increased risk for personal
exposure.
2.7
Schedule
Terracon is prepared to commence work with a minimum of one-week notice. We anticipate a
response time of approximately 72 hours or less for emergency calls, depending on personnel
availability. We anticipate completing routine maintenance events in one day each.
Terracon’s services will be performed in a manner consistent with generally accepted practices of the
professional undertaken in similar studies in the same geographic area during the same period.
Terracon makes no warranties, expressed or implied, regarding its services, findings, conclusions, or
recommendations.

If, as a result of these services, additional work is required outside the scope of this proposal, you will
be contacted, and upon request, proposed costs for additional work will be provided. Authorization will
be obtained prior to commencement of any additional work outside the scope of this proposal.
This proposal and cost estimate was prepared based on the following assumptions:
Client will provide to Terracon, prior to mobilization, legal right of entry to the site (and other
areas if required) to conduct the scope of services.
Client will notify Terracon, prior to mobilization, of any restrictions, special site access
requirements, or known potentially hazardous conditions at the site (e.g., hazardous materials or
processes, specialized protective equipment requirements, unsound structural conditions, etc.)
Replacement materials and equipment to be provided by the Client or billed on a time and
materials basis.
Repairs to the pumps conducted by others, including EPG Companies, will be covered under
warranty of the pumps, or paid by the City of Cheyenne.
Terracon will not be responsible for repairing or replacing equipment if it is damaged as part of
this work scope, with the equipment to be replaced by the City of Cheyenne.
Work can be performed during normal business hours (Monday through Friday, 7:00 am to 7:00
pm).
Traffic control services are not required.
The site is readily accessible by truck.
If any of these assumptions or conditions are not accurate or change during the project, the stated fee
is subject to change. Please contact us immediately if you are aware of any inaccuracies in these
assumptions and conditions, so we may revise the proposal or fee.

Proposal for Leachate System Design, Modification and Maintenance
City of Cheyenne Happy Jack Landfill I Cheyenne, Wyoming
Terracon Proposal No. P20227120 I April 28, 2023
6
4.0 GENERAL COMMENTS
This proposal is valid for 60 days from the date of this proposal. If this proposal meets with your
approval, please provide Terracon with a contract for review. We appreciate the opportunity to provide
this proposal and look forward to collaborating with you on this project. If you have any questions or
comments regarding this proposal, please contact John Skogman at 970-658-4392 or

 ______________________________________________ 
Bid #S-20-23 was solicited for the Greater Cheyenne Greenway, Lions Park Greenway Spot Repair 
project.  A project vicinity map is included for reference. 
 
Six (6) bidders responded to this bid, and the bid tabulation is attached.

a.

line items on Itemized Bid Sheet.

6. 
Knife River.  Cheyenne, WY. 
 
TC Solutions bid was rejected as their Bid Submission contained unit price irregularities.  The lowest 
responsive bidder, is Reiman Corp. 
 
The bids were reviewed by Jeanie Vetter (Planning & Development), Jason Sanchez (Community 
Recreation & Events), and Darci Hendon (Summit Engineering), and their recommendation is to 
accept the lowest responsive bid from Reiman Corp. 
 
The Bid Award amount is requested for $58,282.50.

AGREEMENT 
 
Bid No. S-20-23 
 
Contract No. _______ 
 
THIS AGREEMENT, entered into this ____ day of ___________, _____, by and between 
the CITY OF CHEYENNE, WYOMING, hereinafter referred to as the “CITY”, and Reiman 
Corp., hereinafter referred to as the “CONTRACTOR”. 
 
WITNESSETH that the Contractor and the City, for the considerations stated herein, 
mutually agree as follows: 
 
ARTICLE 1.  STATEMENT OF WORK.  The Contractor shall furnish all supervision, 
technical personnel, labor, materials, machinery, tools, equipment, and services, including utility 
and transportation services, and perform and complete all work in an efficient and workmanlike 
manner in the construction of the “Greater Cheyenne Greenway, Lions Park Greenway Spot 
Repair” project all in strict accordance with the Contract Documents including all addenda 
thereto, numbered and dated: Addendum One (1), dated April 10, 2023. 
 
ARTICLE 2.  RESPONSIBLE DESIGNEE FOR THE CITY.  The Contractor shall in any 
and all matters relating to the scope of services to be provided under this Contract or any other 
provisions herein, contact the City Engineer, or his/her designated representative. 
 
ARTICLE 3.  TIME FRAME FOR COMPLETION.  The services to be performed under 
this Agreement shall commence on the date stipulated in the “Notice to Proceed” that will be issued 
by the City.  The work shall be completed by November 1, 2023.  If the work has not been 
completed within the time stipulated above, including any extensions of time issued by the City 
for excusable delays, the Contractor and his/her sureties shall pay the City fixed, agreed liquidated 
damages, as stipulated in the Supplemental Conditions, for each calendar day of delay until the 
work is completed. 
 
ARTICLE 4.  COMPENSATION AND METHOD OF PAYMENT.  The CITY will pay 
the CONTRACTOR for the performance of the Contract in current funds, the sum of Fifty Eight 
Thousand, Two Hundred Eighty-Two Dollars and Fifty Cents ($58,282.50).  In the event there 
are changes in the estimated quantities shown on the Bid Proposal, the unit prices multiplied by 
the actual quantities shall govern, and the total contract amount will be adjusted accordingly.  The 
City agrees to pay the above amount for contractual services in the following manner, upon receipt 
of appropriate documentation: 
 
a. 
The Contractor will be paid on a monthly basis for percentage of estimated 
work completed.  Submittal will be at least seven (7) business days prior to the payable due 
date as established annually by the City Treasurer’s Office.  The pay request shall be 
submitted on the Contract Payment Request Form and Itemized Pay Request or the AIA 
Documents G702 and G703.  The engineer will review the estimate for approval prior to 
payment. 
5:00 pm, Apr 28 2023

Page 2 of 2 
b. 
The City will withhold five percent (5%) of the dollar value of the work 
completed for a minimum of forty-one (41) calendar days after Notice of Final Settlement 
has been published in accordance with Wyo. Stat. §16-6-116.  Upon completion of the 
work under this Contract, the Contractor shall submit a Contractor’s Certificate of 
Completion; the Consent of Surety; Final Waivers of Lien from the Contractor, and all 
Sub-Contractors, Suppliers and Materialmen; Affidavit of Release of Liens; Affidavit of 
Payment; and a current Workers Compensation Certificate.  Final payment will not be 
made until the above documents have been received by the City and all items on the Punch 
List have been completed, and the advertising requirements have been met. 
 
ARTICLE 5.  CONTRACT.  The executed Contract Documents shall consist of the 
following: 
 
1. This Agreement;  
2. Addenda; 
3. Invitation for Bids; 
4. Instructions to Bidders; 
5. Signed Bid Proposal; 
 
6. General Conditions and Insurance; 
7. Supplemental Conditions; 
8. Part IV Forms & Notices; 
9. Specifications and Special 
Provisions; and  
10. Drawings. 
 
This Agreement, together with other documents enumerated in this Article 5, which said 
other documents are as fully a part of the Contract as if hereto attached or herein repeated, forms 
the Contract between the parties hereto. 
 
IN WITNESS WHEREOF, THAT the governing body of the City of Cheyenne has 
authorized the Mayor as Executive Officer of the City to enter into this Agreement, and that the 
parties hereto have caused this Agreement to be executed on the day and year in the first part herein 
written.

FROM:

SUBJECT: 
Consideration to Purchase between the City of Cheyenne, and Power Equipment 
Company for the repair of a Paving Machine for the Street and Alley Department 
______________________________________________ 
This is a Sole Source purchase through Power Equipment Company, therefore no bids were 
requested. 
Power Equipment Company is the Sole Source authorized dealer for sales and service for Cedar 
Rapids paving machines, therefore repairs must be made directly through them.  
Repairs to the paving machine include replacing strike offs, screed plates, flange bearing, hour 
meter, tachometer, and full machine check over to ensure unit is prepared for paving season. 
The current repair estimate is $44,579.18.  The City recommends approval of a 15% Contingency 
($6,686.88) in the event unexpected items need to be addressed during the course of repairs.  Total 
requested amount for repairs, is a not-to-exceed amount of $51,266.06.

WITNESSETH that the Contractor and the City, for the considerations stated herein, 
mutually agree as follows: 
 
ARTICLE 1.  STATEMENT OF WORK.  The Contractor shall furnish all supervision, 
technical personnel, labor, materials, machinery, tools, equipment, and services, including utility 
and transportation services, and perform and complete all work in an efficient and workmanlike 
manner in the construction of the “2023 Crack Seal #1” project all in strict accordance with the 
Contract Documents including all addenda thereto, numbered and dated: None. 
 
ARTICLE 2.  RESPONSIBLE DESIGNEE FOR THE CITY.  The Contractor shall in any 
and all matters relating to the scope of services to be provided under this Contract or any other 
provisions herein, contact the City Engineer, or his/her designated representative. 
 
ARTICLE 3.  TIME FRAME FOR COMPLETION.  The services to be performed under 
this Agreement shall commence on the date stipulated in the “Notice to Proceed” that will be issued 
by the City.  The work shall be completed by September 30, 2023.  If the work has not been 
completed within the time stipulated above, including any extensions of time issued by the City 
for excusable delays, the Contractor and his/her sureties shall pay the City fixed, agreed liquidated 
damages, as stipulated in the Supplemental Conditions, for each calendar day of delay until the 
work is completed. 
 
ARTICLE 4.  COMPENSATION AND METHOD OF PAYMENT.  The CITY will pay 
the CONTRACTOR for the performance of the Contract in current funds, the sum of Four 
Hundred Forty-Eight Thousand, Two Hundred Sixty-Five Dollars, and Twenty-Six Cents 
($448,265.26).  In the event there are changes in the estimated quantities shown on the Bid 
Proposal, the unit prices multiplied by the actual quantities shall govern, and the total contract 
amount will be adjusted accordingly.  The City agrees to pay the above amount for contractual 
services in the following manner, upon receipt of appropriate documentation: 
 
a. 
The Contractor will be paid on a monthly basis for percentage of estimated 
work completed.  Submittal will be at least seven (7) business days prior to the payable due 
date as established annually by the City Treasurer’s Office.  The pay request shall be 
submitted on the Contract Payment Request Form and Itemized Pay Request or the AIA 
Documents G702 and G703.  The engineer will review the estimate for approval prior to 
payment.

completed for a minimum of forty-one (41) calendar days after Notice of Final Settlement

work under this Contract, the Contractor shall submit a Contractor’s Certificate of 
Completion; the Consent of Surety; Final Waivers of Lien from the Contractor, and all 
Sub-Contractors, Suppliers and Materialmen; Affidavit of Release of Liens; Affidavit of 
Payment; and a current Workers Compensation Certificate.  Final payment will not be 
made until the above documents have been received by the City and all items on the Punch

# SOURCE_FILE: CHEY-2023-05-02_TotalExtraction_59870545.pdf
MEMORANDUM 
TO: 
 
Mayor Patrick Collins, and City Council 
FROM: 
 
TJ Barttelbort, Purchasing Manager 
DATE: 
 
May 16, 2023 
SUBJECT: 
Consideration to Purchase between the City of Cheyenne, and Xerographic 
Equipment Systems, Inc. (XESI), to cancel existing copier leases and to purchase 
eighteen (18) new copiers 
______________________________________________ 
This is a Single Source purchase through Xerographic Equipment Systems, Inc. (XESI), therefore 
no bids were requested. 
The City currently has existing leases for the eighteen (18) copiers being considered at this time.   
A cost-benefit analysis has been completed on owning versus leasing the copier equipment, and it 
has been determined that there is a potential for cost savings by purchasing the copiers and 
abandoning the leases.  The break-even cost savings point is at year seven (7), when a $18,442.18 
cost savings will be realized.  Every year following year seven (7), the City will continue to save 
money by owning the copiers rather than leasing. 
The basis for this Single Source purchase, is that XESI is allowing the City to terminate all eighteen 
(18) existing leases at no cost to the City.  This penalty-free lease termination is only authorized 
by purchasing the eighteen (18) new copiers outright from XESI.  Due to the existing contractual 
relationship with XESI, they are the only vendor that can terminate the leases penalty-free.  If the 
City were to terminate the existing leases and purchase the copiers from another vendor, the 
penalties and fees would eliminate any potential for cost saving. 
The total requested purchase amount is $202,990.96.   
Funding is provided via one (1) time distribution dollars from the State of Wyoming. 
 
Funding: 
001-21-2113-40-86936  
 


REQUEST TO PURCHASE OVER $75,000.00 
Consideration to Purchase between the City of Cheyenne, and Xerographic Equipment Systems, 
Inc. (XESI), to cancel existing copier leases and to purchase eighteen (18) new copiers.  This is 
a single source purchase, therefore no bids were requested.  The total requested amount is 
$202,990.96.   
 
This will be reviewed by the City Council Finance Committee on June 5, 2023 and voted on at 
the June 12, 2023 City Council meeting.

In this Cost Per Print Agreement, as it may be amended or supplemented from time to time (the “Agreement”), the word “Customer” 
means the Customer named above.  “Company” means Xerographic Equipment Systems, Inc., an authorized Xerox® Managed Print 
Services channel partner. “Xerox” means Xerox Corporation.   
 
1. SERVICES. COMPANY OR XEROX WILL PROVIDE THE SERVICES (“SERVICES”) AS DESCRIBED ON ONE OR MORE 
ATTACHED PRINT SERVICE SCHEDULES(S) (“SCHEDULES”) FOR THE DEVICES ON SUCH SCHEDULES (“MANAGED 
DEVICES”). The Tools (as defined below) will be used to monitor the networked Managed Devices. The Tools provide automatic service 
and supplies alerts to the Help Desk (e.g. low toner) and are used for automated meter collection, new device discovery, and device data 
collection for reporting. The Tools periodically scan approved ranges of Customer’s network for devices which may be eligible to be added 
as Managed Devices. Devices identified through this process may be added as Managed Devices and billed at the rates identified in the 
Schedules.  Customer agrees to pay to Company the “Minimum Monthly Charges” set forth on Schedules, any excess print charges, and 
all other amounts stated herein.       
2. TERM AND TERMINATION.  The term of this Agreement will begin on the date Company accepts and signs the Agreement and will 
continue for as long as devices are covered by a Schedule to this Agreement (“Term”). Each Schedule has its own term (“Schedule 
Term”) for the Managed Devices covered by such Schedule.  Company may terminate any Managed Device covered by this Agreement 
at the end of a Schedule Term if Xerox gives Company notice that such device is no longer supported.  Each Managed Device covered 
by a Schedule to this Agreement will automatically renew for additional one-year terms unless either party provides the other party with 
written notice of non-renewal at least 45 days, but not more than 90 days, before the end of the existing Schedule Term. Upon Customer’s 
request, Company will provide Customer with revised pricing for the renewal term within 90 days before the end of the existing Schedule 
Term for the Managed Devices that will be supported at the time of renewal and will use reasonable efforts to notify Customer of the 
devices that are no longer supported.    The annual price increase shall not exceed 15%. 
3. BREAK FIX SERVICES. Except for Managed Devices identified as “Supplies Only” on the Schedules, Xerox will keep the Managed 
Devices in good working order ("Break Fix Services"). 
a) If a device fault cannot be resolved remotely, Company will dispatch a service technician to perform on-site Break Fix Services, 
which will usually be the next business day. On-site Break Fix Services are provided Monday through Friday (excluding New Year’s 
Day, Memorial Day, Independence Day, Labor Day, Thanksgiving Day and Christmas Day) from 8:00AM to 5:00PM local time 
(“Service Hours”). Break-Fix Services exclude repairs due to: (i) misuse, neglect, abuse or operation of a Managed Device outside 
the original equipment manufacturers (“OEM”) specifications; (ii) failure of the Customer’s PC to comply with the OEM’s published 
specifications; (iii) act of God or other force majeure event; (iv) relocation, alterations, or use of options, accessories, service or 
supplies not provided by Company; (v) failure to perform any Customer Responsibilities identified in the section titled “Customer 
Responsibilities”; or (vi) acts or omissions of Customer or any party not affiliated with Company or Xerox.  
4. SUPPLIES. Xerox/Company will furnish toner or ink (“Supplies”), as applicable, for Managed Devices. 
a)  Supplies requests may be placed by the Tools. 
b) Supplies are Xerox’s property until used by Customer, and Customer will use them only with the Managed Devices. Selling, 
transferring, bartering, or otherwise conveying Supplies to anyone is strictly prohibited and is subject to civil and criminal penalties. 
12:04 pm, May 16 2023

 
5.  HELP DESK. The Help Desk is available during the Service Hours to receive, track, escalate, process and close service issues, 
remotely resolve certain issues, and process Customer requests for Break Fix Services.  
 
6. CUSTOMER RESPONSIBILITIES. Customer will: 
a) Notify Company if Customer wishes to relocate a Managed Device, and Company will advise Customer if Services are available 
at the new location. 
b) Assure that networked Managed Devices are Simple Network Management Protocol (“SNMP”) enabled and can route SNMP over 
the network.  If meter reads are not provided, Company (i) may estimate the number of prints used and invoice Customer accordingly, 
(ii) may charge a fee in the amount of $_50.00__ (if left blank the amount is $50) per Device that do not report meter readings for 
each month until meter readings are provided, and (iii) will adjust the estimated charge for excess prints upon receipt of actual meter 
readings. 
c) Provide a dedicated PC (or server) that is always connected to Customer’s network (“Customer PC”); and allow Company to 
install, use, access, update and maintain the Tools on the Customer PC. The Tools cannot be installed on a PC where other SNMP-
based applications or other Xerox Tools are installed, because they may interfere with the Tools. 
7. PRINT CHARGES; LATE FEES AND TAXES.  Each month, Customer agrees to pay Company, by the due date as set forth on the 
invoice, all Minimum Monthly Charges, excess print charges and all other amounts due under this Agreement (including all applicable 
taxes). Customer agrees to pay the Minimum Monthly Charges each month even if Customer does not make the number of 
prints included with the Minimum Monthly Charge.  Customer may not carry over a credit from any month during which 
Customer makes fewer than the minimum number of prints.   If Company does not receive payment in full on or before its due date, 
Customer shall pay (i) a fee equal to the greater of 1.5% of the amount that is late or $25, plus (ii) interest on the part of the payment that 
is late in the amount of 1.5% per month from the due date to the date paid.  Customer is responsible for all taxes (including, without 
limitation, sales, use and personal property taxes, and excluding only taxes based on Company’s income), levies, assessments, license 
and registration fees and other governmental charges relating to this Agreement (collectively, “Taxes”).  Company may periodically bill 
Customer for, and Customer agrees to promptly pay any Taxes. Customer authorizes Company to pay any Taxes when and as they may 
become due, and Customer agrees to reimburse Company promptly upon demand for the full amount (less any estimated amounts 
previously paid by Customer). 
8. LOSS AND DAMAGE. Customer bears the risk of loss and damage to the Managed Devices and Customer shall continue to perform 
its obligations even if it becomes damaged or suffers a loss.

To Whom it May Concern. 
 
The City of Cheyenne has the following leases listed below with Xerox Financial Services.  The City of 
Cheyenne is planning to replace these copiers with new Xerox devices purchased machines from XESI. At 
the Ɵme of delivery of the new Xerox machines, the City of Cheyenne will no longer be liable for any 
future lease payments on the leases listed below.  The machines will be picked up and sent back to Xerox 
Financial Services at no cost to the City of Cheyenne.  If the following leased devices are replaced by 
another vendor the lease terminaƟon fees and return fees with be billed to the City of Cheyenne. 
 
 
Sincerely, 
Derek G. Eskam

17th April 2023
Mayor Patrick Collins & City Council Members
2101 O’Neil Avenue
Cheyenne, WY 82001
Dear Mr. Collins and Members,
We are excited to be applying for a Restaurant Liquor License with the City of
Cheyenne. It is no small task opening a business, even an existing one but we
see what L’Osteria Mondello can be in the future. Mostly known now as one of
the local favorite pizzerias, we plan to open Cheyenne to the fine dining and
cultural experiences it deserves. With its long standing history in the
Cheyenne community and its roots in Italian culture, L’Osteria Mondello plans
to bring an array of experiences from Italy and the Mediterranean atmosphere
with beverages to complement these dishes.
With the ability to serve alcoholic beverages our dishes will be nicely balanced
and our menus well rounded. So we hope you will consider our application
and help our small business grow and continue to serve this community we
call home.
Sincerely,
Anika Willaims

This Lease Agreement made and entered into effective as of the 1st day of July,
2023 (the “Effective Date”). by and between Greenway Commons LLC, hereinafter
called “Landlord”, and Stella Polare LLC, hereinafter called “Tenant.”
WITNESSETH:
in consideration of mutual covenants and agreements contained herein, and for
other good and valuable consideration. which are hereby acknowledged. Landlord hereby
leases to Tenant and Tenant rents from Landlord, those certain premises set forth below
(“Leased Premises”) and Tenant does hereby agree to the following:
ARTICLE I
LEASED PREMISES, TERM, AND RENT
SECTION 1.1 LEASED PREMISES AND TERM
(a) Premises: Landlord hereby leases to Tenant and Tenant hereby leases from
Landlord, upon the terms and conditions hereinafter set forth, Suite D in the Grcenwav
Plaza Shopping Center Located at
1 507 Stillwater Avenue. Cheyenne, Wyoming 82009
consisting of approximately 3,400 square feet (hereinafter referred to as the “Leased
Premises”).
(h) Term: The initial term of this Lease shall be for a period of Five (5) years.
commencing on the Effective Date of this Lease and piring on June 30, 2028.
Tenant shall have the right and option to extend the ‘Term 01’ this Lease for one
additional three (3) year term, which extension term shall commence upon the expiration
of the original Term of this Lease. If Tenant exercises its option to extend the Term, the
extension shall he upon the same terms. conditions, and covenants of this Lease except
the annual rental payable during the extended Term shall be increased in an amount that
vi11 be determined at the time Tenant exercises its option. If Tenant desires to exercise its
option to extend the Term, the Tenant shall give written notice to the Landlord one
hundred twenty (120) days prior to the expiration of the Term of this Lease. If said notice
is not received by Landlord within said one hundred twenty (120) day time period.
Landlord shall have no obligation to renew the lease. Upon receipt of Tenant’s notice to
exercise its option. Landlord and Tenant agree to negotiate a new lease rate. Should the
parties not be able to conic to an agreement on the new lease rate, upon expiration of the
initial term, this Lease shall terminate.
(c) Annual Rent: The annual rent payable by Tenant during the first two (2) years
of this Lease shall be Fifty One Thousand and No/lO() Dollars (S5l.000.00) per year for
the initial term of the Lease, payable in monthly installments of Four Thousand Two
Hundred Fifty and No/100 Dollars ($4,250.00). Beginning in year three of this Lease. on
July 1, 2025, Rent shall increase One Hundred Dollars (Sl00.00) per month for an annual
total rent of Fifty Two Thousand Two Hundred and No/lOO Dollars ($52,200.00).

Beginning in year five of this Lease, on July 1, 2027, Rent shall increase by another One
Hundred Dollars (S 100.00) per month for an annual total rent of Fitly Three Thousand
Four Hundred and No/l0() Dollars ($53,400.00). Tenant shall pay monthly installments
of rent to Landlord without previous demand therefore and without any setoft’ or
deduction whatsoever, on the first day of each and every calendar month throughout the
Term and any extension thereof, and. if the term commences on a date other than the first
day of a month) Tenant shall pay Landlord on the first day of the Term, a pro-rata portion
of such Annual Rent: calculated on a thirty (30) day calendar month. Tenant shall pay an
additional monthly amount of Four Hundred and No/l00 Dollar (S400.00) for common
area expenses as provided for in Section 2.2 below.
The combined monthly amounts
shall make Tenant’s total monthly payment shall he Four Thousand Six Hundred Fifty
and No/l00 ($4,650.00).
(d) Condition of Building: Tenant shall receive the Leased Premises in its current
condition, ‘as is.”
(e) Security Deposit: Tenant shall pay a security deposit in the amount of Three
Thousand Dollars (S3.000.00). This deposit will be refunded at the expiration of the
Lease Term to Tenant so long as Tenant is not in default under the terms of this
Agreement, less any amounts applied against any expenses the Landlord incurs
in
cleaning or repairing the Leased Premises, (ordinary wear and tear excepted), against the
cost to professionally clean the carpets upon move out, against any rent which Tenant
fails to pay. late fees owed by Tenant, and/or damage to the Premises caused or
contributed to by the Tenant. The return of the security deposit is also conditioned on the
Leased Premises being returned to Landlord in a clean and tenantable condition.
(f) Notice Address: If to Tenant: Stella Polare LLC, 3704 Horse Creek Rd.,
Cheyenne. Wyoming 82009. If to Landlord: Greenway Commons LLC. 109 E. 17
St..
Cheyenne, Wyoming 82001. If either of the parties’ address changes, they shall give the
other party written notice of such change within five (5) days.

PREMISES

ARTICLE Ii
COMMON AREA, ASSOCIATION, AND OTHER CHARGES
SECTION 2.1 TAXES
The Tenant’s share of real property taxes for the Leased Premises are included in
the base rent for the Leased Premises: however, Tenant shall be responsible to pay any
and all personal property taxes during the term of the Lease for personal property owned
by the Tenant and kept on the Leased Premises.
SECTION 2.2 COMMON AREAS AND OPERATING COSTS
All common areas and oilier common facilities (hereinafter collectively called
“Common Areas”) made available by Landlord shall he subject to the exclusive control
and management of Landlord. Common Areas (as initially constructed or as the same
may he enlarged or reduced at any time thereafter) shall mean all areas, space. facilities.
equipment, signs and special services from time to time made available by Landlord for
the common and joint use and benefit of Landlord, the Tenant and other tenants and

of

and discharge all personnel with respect thereto.

areas be deemed a constructive or actual eviction. However, the building and common
areas cannot be changed in a manner which would substantially affect Tenant’s ability to

operate its business. Landlord reserves the right to grant to third persons the non-
exclusive right to cross over and USC in common with Landlord and all tenants of the

Tenant hereby agrees to pay Landlord a share, computed as hereinafter provided,

building. “Operating Costs” shall mean the total cost and expense incurred in operating.
maintaining and repairing the common areas, including without limitation, surcharges

Tenant’s share of such operating costs shall be pro-rated for the common areas

Landlord has estimated the Operating Costs referred to in this section at S4,800

Should there be any
surplus in the Operating Costs and described for herein. Tenant shall receive a credit

(h) All garbage and refuse shall he kept inside the Leased Premises in the kind of

SECTION 3.3 SIGNS, AWNINGS AND CANOPIES
Landlord may erect and maintain such suitable signs as it in its sole discretion

SECTION 8.1 DEFAULT

expenses to cover Landlord’s overhead in making such repairs on Tenant’s behalf: Tenant
shall not remove any plumbing or electrical fixtures or equipment, heating or air
condition equipment, floor coverings (including, but not limited to. vall to ‘all
carpeting) walls or ceilings, all of which shall be deemed to constitute a part of the
Leased Premises owned by Landlord, nor shall Tenant remove any fixtures or machinery
that were lurnished or paid for by Landlord (whether initially installed or replaced). The
Leased Premises shall he left in a broom-clean condition. If Tenant shall fail to remove
its trade fixtures or other property as provided in this Section 9.6, such fixtures and other
property not removed by Tenant shall he deemed abandoned by Tenant and at the option
of Landlord shall become the property of Landlord, or at Landlord’s option
may be
renim’ed by Landlord at Tenant’s expense plus twenty percent (20%) as herein provided.
or placed in storage at Tenant’s expense, or sold. or otherwise disposed of at Landlord’s
sole and absolute discretion. in which event the proceeds of such sale or other disposition
shall belong to Landlord. Notwithstanding anything to the contrary contained herein. any
of the improvements or alterations made by Tenant which are not moveahic without
damaging the building shall be deemed to be made part of the Leased Premises and shall
become property of the Landlord at the termination of this Lease.
SECTION 9.7 LIENS
Tenant shall discharge any lien filed against the building as a result of Tenant’s
actions or any part thereof for work done or materials furnished with respect to the
Leased Premises within thirty (30) days after such lien is filed and Tenant shall execute
lien waivers prior to commencement of’ construction in the Leased Premises. If Tenant
fails to keep this covenant, in addition to any other remedies available to Landlord under
this Lease or otherwise, Landlord may at its option discharge such lien. in which event
Tenant agrees to pay Landlord a sum equal to the amount of the lien thus discharged, plus
19

Landlord’s internal administrative costs, attorney’s fees, expenses and damages thereby
caused Landlord.
SECTION 9.8 INTEREST
Whenever this Lease refers to “Interest”, the rate shall be ten percent (10%).
SECTION 9.9 LATE PAYMENTS
Should Tenant fail to pay when due any installment of Annual Rent or any other
sum payable to Landlord under the tern-is of this Lease, then Interest shall accrue from the
date of such delinquency and shall continue until such sum is paid. and such lnteres
together with a Late Charge of One Hundred Dollars ($100.00) shall he paid by Tenant to
Landlord at the lime of payment of the delinquent sum.
SECTION 9.10 CONSENTS
With respect to any provision of this Lease which either provides or is held to
provide that consent is required, Landlord or Tenant shall not unreasonably withhold or
unreasonably delay any consent or approval.
SECTION 9.11 NOTICES
Whenever notice shall or may be given to either of the parties by the other, each
such notice shall be by registered or certifIed mail with return receipt requested to the
respective address of the party to receive notice as contained herein or to such other
address as either party may from lime to time designate in writing to the other. Any
notice under this Lease shall be deemed to have been given at the time it is placed in the
mail with sufficient postage prcpaid.
SECTiON 9.12 NO BROKER
Tenant warrants and represents that no broker was involved on its behalf in
negotiating or consummating this Lease and agrees to indemnify and hold Landlord
harmless from and against any and all claims for brokerage commissions arising out of
any communications or negotiations had by Tenant with any broker regarding the Leased
Premises or any other premises in the building and/or the conSummation of this Lease.
Landlord is solely responsible for the payment of any broker’s commission.
SECTION 9.13 SHORT FORM LEASE
Tenant agrees not to record this Lease without the express written consent of
Landlord and further agrees to execute, acknowledge and deliver at any time after the
20

date of this Lease, at the request of Landlord, a “short form lease” or “memorandum of
lease” suitable for recording.
SECTION 9.14 ENTIRE AND BINDING AGREEMENT
This Business Lease Agreement contains all of the agreements between the
parties hereto and it may not he modified in any manner other than by agreement in
writing signed by all the parties hereto or their successors in interest. The terms,
covenants and conditions contained herein shall inure to the benefit of and he binding
upon Landlord and Tenant and their respective successors and assigns, except as may be
otherwise expressly provided in this Lease. Tenant acknowledges that neither Landlord
nor any broker has made any representation to or agreement with Tenant which is not
contained in this Lease.
SECTION 9.15 PROVISIONS SEVERABLE
If any term or provision of this Lease or the application thereof to any person or
circumstance shall, to any extent. be invalid or unenforceable, the remainder of’ this lease,
or the application of such tenn or provision to persons or circumstances other than those
as to which it is held invalid or unenforceable, shall not be affected thereby and each term
and provision of this Lease shall be valid and be enforced to the fullest extent permitted
by law.
SECTION 9.16 CAPTIONS
The captions contained herein are for the convenience and reference only and
shall not he deemed as part of this Lease or construed as in any manner limiting or
amplifying the terms and provisions of this Lease to which they relate.
